ORDINANCE NO. PHSD 01 -09 
 
Adopted March 10, 2003 
Amended June 9, 2009, by Ordinance No. 01-09 
Amended August 2022 by Ordinance No. 01-09 
 
Chapter 1 Legislative Authority 
 
Reg. 1-1-101 Legislative Authority 
A. Board of Supervisors 
1. The Board of Supervisors has authority pursuant to A.R.S. §11-251(17) to 
“adopt provisions necessary to preserve the health of the county and provide for 
the expenses thereof.” 
2. The Board of Supervisors is further authorized, pursuant to A.R.S. §11-
251(31), to “make and enforce all local, police, sanitary and other regulations 
not in conflict with general law.” 
3. The Board of Supervisors is required to “…establish a county department of 
health or a public health services district,” pursuant to A.R.S. §36-182(A). 
B. Board of Health 
1. The Board of Health is charged with making “rules and regulations, not 
inconsistent with the rules and regulations of the department of health services, 
for the protection and preservation of public health,” pursuant to A.R.S. §36-
184(B)(3). 
2. The Board of Health is also charged with “recommending rules and regulations 
to the respective county Boards of Supervisors for adoption and enforcement in 
their respective counties,” pursuant to A.R.S. §36-184(B)(5). 
C. Director of a County Health Department: the director of a county health department is 
mandated to “enforce and observe the rules of the director of the department of health 
services, the director of the department of environmental quality and the local board of 
health, county rules and regulations concerning health, and laws of the state pertaining 
to the preservation of public health and protection of the environment,” pursuant to 
A.R.S. §36-186(5). 
D. Delegation 
1. The director of the Arizona Department of Health Services is authorized, 
pursuant to A.R.S. §36-136(E), to “…delegate to a local health 
department…any functions, powers or duties that the director believes can be 
competently, efficiently and properly performed by the local health 
department...” 
2. The delegation was executed by the director of the Arizona Department of 
Health Services and the Navajo County Board of Supervisors. 
3. A copy of the delegation agreement is on file at the Department's 
Environmental Health Office. 
E. U.S. Food and Drug Administration Voluntary Nation Retail Food Regulatory Program 
Standards 
1. Program designed to help facilitate and enhance the services offered by the 
Department to the public.  
i. Promote wider application of effective risk-factor intervention 
strategies;

<!-- page 158 -->
4 
 
ii. Assist in identifying program areas most in need; 
iii. Provide information to justify maintenance or increase in program 
budgets. 
iv. Lead to innovations in program implementation and administration; and 
v. Improve industry and consumer confidence in food protection by 
enhancing uniformity within and between regulatory agencies.  
F. Schedule of Fees 
1. A.RS. § 36-187 authorizes the Board to adopt a schedule of reasonable fees to 
be charged by the District for issuing or renewing licenses or permits and 
performing other statutory and regulatory duties. 
2. This ordinance establishes the fees to be charged by the District for (1) 
reviewing plans and specifications for regulated Food Establishments, and (2) 
issuing and renewing Licenses to Operate to regulated Food Establishments, 
Public and Semipublic Bathing Places, Water Haulers and Septic Haulers. It 
also specifies late charges for failure to pay such fees when due and authorizes 
the District to take enforcement action to ensure payment. The fees established 
herein are based on the expenses incurred by the District in administering the 
state statutes and regulations and performing related reviews and inspections.  
3. Table 1 documents the Schedule of Fees. 
G. Jurisdiction 
This ordinance applies throughout Navajo County (including incorporated cities and towns), with the 
exception of land under Tribal sovereignty.

<!-- page 159 -->
5 
 
 
 
Chapter 2 Establishments 
 
Reg. 1-2-101 Definitions 
A. “Annual Operating License” means a document of written approval from the 
Department to operate an establishment for twelve (12) months beginning on January 
31
st. 
B. “Approved” means acceptable to the Health Officer. 
C. “Board of Health” means the Navajo County Board of Health as established pursuant 
to A.R.S § 36-183. 
D. “Beverage” means a liquid for drinking, including water.  
E. “Caterer” or “Food caterer” means a person who prepares food at an approved 
commissary for delivery or services at a single location for a predetermined number of 
people and consists of at least one food item that will be cooked and/or served at the 
delivery location.  
F. “Certified Manager” means a food establishment employee who has successfully 
completed a manager’s training course approved by the Conference for Food 
Protection. 
G. “Commissary” means a fixed and licensed food establishment that acts as a base of 
operation for another food operation or food vendor. 
H. “Core Item” means an item not designated as a priority item or priority foundation item 
and includes an item that usually relates to general sanitation, operational controls, 
sanitation standard operating procedures (SSOPs), facilities or structures, equipment 
design, or general maintenance. 
I. “Delivery Location” means the site to which the caterer transports the food from the 
commissary for limited preparation, cooking, and service.  
J. “Department” means the Navajo County Public Health Services District. 
K. “Food” means a raw, cooked, or processed edible substance, ice, beverage, or 
ingredient used or intended for use or for sale in whole or in part for human 
consumption or chewing gum. 
L. Food Establishment 
1. “Food Establishment” means an operation that: 
a. Stores, prepares, packages, serves, vends food directly to the consumer, or 
otherwise provides food for human consumption such as a restaurant; 
satellite or catered feeding location; catering operation if the operation 
provides food directly to a consumer or to a conveyance used to transport 
people; market; vending location; conveyance used to transport people; 
institution; or food bank; and 
b. Relinquishes possession of food to a consumer directly, or indirectly 
through a delivery service such as home delivery of grocery orders or 
restaurant takeout orders, or delivery service that is provided by common 
carriers. 
2. “Food Establishment” includes: 
a. Any element or aspect of the operation such as a transportation 
vehicle or a central preparation facility that supplies a vending 
location or satellite feeding location unless the vending or feeding 
location is independently licensed and permitted by the regulatory 
authority; and

<!-- page 160 -->
6 
 
b. An operation that is conducted in a mobile, stationary, temporary, or 
permanent facility or location where consumption is on or off the 
premises, regardless of whether there is a charge for the food. 
3. “Food Establishment” does not include the following: 
a. An establishment that offers only prepackaged food that is not 
time/temperature control for safety food. 
b. A produce stand that only offers whole, uncut fresh fruits and vegetables. 
c. A kitchen in a private home if only food that is not time/temperature 
control for safety food is prepared for sale or service at a function such as 
a legally permissible religious or charitable organization’s bake sale and 
there is a clearly visible placard at the sales and service location advising 
consumer(s) that the food is prepared in a kitchen that is not subject to 
regulation and inspection by the regulatory authority; 
d. An area where food is prepared in accordance with subparagraph (3)(c) of 
this definition is sold or offered for human consumption; 
e. A kitchen in a private home such as a small family day-care provider; or a 
bed-and-breakfast operation that prepares and offers food to guests if the 
home is owner occupied, the number of available guest bedrooms does not 
exceed six (6), breakfast is the only meal offered, the number of guests 
served does not exceed eighteen (18), and the consumer is informed by 
statements contained in published advertisements, mailed brochures, and 
placards posted at the registration area that the food is prepared in a 
kitchen that is not regulated and inspected by the regulatory authority; or 
f. A private home that receives catered or home-delivered food. 
4. “Risk Type I Food Establishment” means a food establishment that: 
a. Only makes available or serves food that is time/temperature control for 
safety food that is commercially prepackaged in an approved processing 
facility; or 
b. Only makes available or serves a limited preparation of food, including 
food establishments that only serve or make available beverages, that is 
not time/temperature control for safety food. 
5. “Risk Type II Food Establishment” means a food service that requires 
time/temperature control for safety food as follows: 
a. Food is prepared from raw ingredients which require only minimal 
assembly; or 
b. Hot or cold preparation of food is restricted to same-day service; or 
c. Food requiring preparation is obtained from an approved processing 
facility. 
6. “Risk Type III Food Establishment” means a food service that requires 
time/temperature control for safety food as follows: 
a. Hot hold or cold hold food prepared more than twelve (12) hours before 
service; or 
b. Cooking and cooling food is part of the handling process; or 
c. Vacuumed packaging and other forms of reduced oxygen packaging are 
performed at the retail level; or 
d. Food is prepared for a highly susceptible population. 
I. “Food Worker” means any person who handles, prepares, serves, sells, or gives away 
food for consumption or any person who handles utensils and equipment pertaining to

<!-- page 161 -->
7 
 
food services. The term does not include persons in food establishments regulated 
under the Health Code who handle food or drink exclusively in closed crates, cartons, 
packages, bottles, or similar containers in which no portion of the food or drink is 
exposed to contamination through such handling. 
J. “Health Code” or “Navajo County Health Code” means Navajo County Health Code 
and Ordinance 01-09. 
K. “Health Inspector” means an Environmental Health Specialist, Sanitarian, Sanitarian 
Aide, or Sanitarian Trainee, employed by the Department. 
L. “Health Officer” means the Director or his/her authorized representative. 
M. “Hearing Officer” means an individual that has been appointed by the Health Officer 
and then approved by the Board of Health to conduct an administrative hearing 
regarding the denial, suspension, or revocation of a license or permit. 
N. “Hot Water” means potable water under pressure at a minimum of 110° F at the hot 
water spigot. 
O. “Interfere” means obstructing or prohibiting the performance of a duty, giving physical 
or verbal abuse, using threatening actions or statements, ordering or demanding that the 
Health Officer or Health Inspector(s) leave the premises, or refusing the Health Officer 
or Health Inspector(s) access to a premise. 
P. “License” or “Permit” means a written approval issued by the Department that 
authorizes the operation of an establishment or event. 
Q. “Manager” means a person who directs or supervises others or who carries on business 
of the food establishment by whatever title he/she has. 
R. “Mobile Food Establishment” means a food establishment or equipment for a food 
establishment mounted on a motorized vehicle, a trailer, a platform with wheels, or a 
cabinet with wheels, regardless of whether the food establishment is pushed or pulled by 
a motorized vehicle or a person. 
S. “Operation” means performing work either at an establishment or in the field, 
including 1) equipment needed for the work; 2) activities required in the preparation or 
commencement of performing the work; and 3) activities in support of closing and 
clean-up. 
T. “Outdoor” means a delivery location that does not have onsite equipment to maintain 
the catered food at a proper temperature, does not have onsite approved cooking 
equipment, or does not have a structure to protect the catered food from 
contamination.  
U. “Person” means the state, municipality, district, political subdivision, cooperative, 
institution, corporation, company, firm, partnership, association or society, legal entity, 
or individual/natural person. 
V. “Person in Charge” means the individual present at the food establishment who is 
responsible for the food operation at the time of the inspection. 
W. “Pomace” means the pulpy refuse remaining after the juice has been pressed from fruit. 
X. “Premises” means: 
 
1. The physical facility, includes, but is not limited to, place, building, or 
structure; its contents; and the contiguous land or property where business is 
conducted or is under the control of the owner or owner’s representative; or 
2. The physical facility, its contents, and the land or property not described in 
subparagraph (1) of this definition if its facilities and contents are under the control 
of the owner or owner’s representative and may impact food establishment 
personnel, facilities, or operations, and a food establishment is only one component

<!-- page 162 -->
8 
 
of a larger operation such as a health care facility, hotel, motel, school, recreational 
camp, or prison. 
Y. “Priority Item” means a provision in the Health Code whose application contributes 
directly to the elimination, prevention or reduction to an acceptable level, hazards 
associated with foodborne illness or injury and there is no other provision that more 
directly controls the hazard and includes items with a quantifiable measure to show 
control of hazards such as cooking, reheating, cooling, handwashing. 
Z. “Priority Foundation Item” means a provision in the Health Code whose application 
supports, facilitates or enables one or more priority items and includes an item that 
requires the purposeful incorporation of specific actions, equipment or procedures by 
industry management to attain control of risk factors that contribute to foodborne 
illness or injury such as personnel training, infrastructure or necessary equipment, 
Hazard Analysis and Critical Control Point (HACCP) principle plans, food safety 
plans, documentation or record keeping, and labeling. 
AA. “Product” means juice pressed from vegetables, fruits, or any other items that can be 
used for making fermented or unfermented beverages. 
BB. “Processor or Food Processor” means a food business that produces a simple food 
product or a limited food product line for the purpose of human consumption.  
CC. “Reasonable time” means normal operating hours (including the time when employees 
are preparing food or cleaning up) at a food establishment or premises. 
DD. “Seasonal Food” means any food operation that operates at a Farmer’s market for less 
than 6 months of the year at a single event location and is not transferable to any other 
location.  
EE. “Service Organization” means an organization designated as a non-profit by the IRS 
pursuant to Internal Revenue Code Section 501. 
FF. “Temporary Food Establishment” means a food establishment that operates for a 
period of not more than fourteen (14) consecutive days in conjunction with a single 
event or celebration at one location. 
GG. “Temporary Mobile Food Unit” means a mobile food establishment that operates in 
conjunction with a Special Event, including, but not limited to, a fair, a rodeo, an 
exhibition, or a similar event for not more than fourteen (14) consecutive days at the 
same location for the same event.  
HH. “Time/temperature control for safety food" means a food that requires 
time/temperature control for safety (TCS) to limit pathogenic microorganism growth or 
toxin formation. 
 
Reg. 1-2-201 Application for a License or Permit 
a) It is the responsibility of the owner or owner’s representative, to obtain a license or permit 
from the Department to operate an establishment listed in Table 1 of the Health Code. A 
person who is convicted for the failure to obtain or retain a license or permit is guilty of a 
Class 2 misdemeanor.  
b) An application for a license or permit to operate an establishment in Navajo County, which is 
under the jurisdiction of the Department, must be submitted on a form provided by and 
approved by the Department.  
i) The application form must be complete.  
ii) Information provided on the application form must be accurate and legible. 
c) The Department will review the application for completeness.

<!-- page 163 -->
9 
 
d) The Department will request missing information, and if the requested missing information is 
not provided, the Department may reject the application and the license or permit may be 
denied.   
e) License or Permit Fee  
i) The license or permit fees and other applicable fees must be submitted with the 
application.  
ii) All license or permit fees and other applicable fees may be paid by check, money order, or 
credit card.  
iii) All license or permit fees and other applicable fees are non-refundable unless approved by 
the Health Director.  
iv) Returned checks will be assessed a late fee, a returned check fee of $25, and any legal fees 
assessed by the County Attorney’s office.  
 
Reg. 1-2-202 License or Permit Types 
a) Annual Operating License 
i) Prior to operating an establishment, the owner or owner’s representative must apply for 
and obtain an annual operating license. 
ii) Application for an annual operating license must be submitted to the Department, which 
has the authority to either approve or deny the application. 
iii) The applicant will be notified by the Department of its decision to approve or deny the 
application for an annual operating license. 
iv) The annual operating license must be posted in a conspicuous location and be easily 
observed by consumer(s) and Health Inspector(s). 
b) Temporary Food or Seasonal Food License 
i) A Temporary Food or Seasonal Food license application must be approved by the 
Department before a person may provide food and/or beverage(s) at an event or 
celebration. Application must be received by the Department at least twenty-one (21) days 
before the event. If an application for a special event license is received less than 21 days 
before the event, late fees will be assessed. 
ii) A Temporary Food or Seasonal Food license is valid for the duration of the event unless it 
is suspended or revoked. Notwithstanding the foregoing, a special event license may not 
be issued for a period of more than fourteen (14) consecutive days. 
iii) A non-refundable fee, including any applicable late fees, for the special event license must 
be paid when the application for a Temporary Food or Seasonal Food license is submitted 
to the Department. 
c) Special Event Coordinator License 
i) Event coordinators for temporary events including parades, farmer’s markets, street fairs, 
carnivals, and similar events that operate for up to fourteen (14) consecutive day shall 
apply six (6) weeks prior to the scheduled event and should include: 
(1) Application (no fee assessed); and 
(2) A list of all food establishments. 
 
 
d) Waiver 
i) Fees imposed by this Ordinance may be waived if the Health Officer is satisfied that the 
applicant is a bona fide non-profit entity. An applicant requesting a waiver shall provide 
all such information as the Health Officer may reasonably require in order to verify the 
applicant's non-profit status. In times of hardship, an applicant can request a waive.

<!-- page 164 -->
10 
 
Reg. 1-2-203 Renewal of Annual Operating License 
a) It is the responsibility of the owner or owner’s representative to renew the annual operating 
license on or before the expiration date. 
b) The renewal application must be approved before the license will be issued. The application 
must be complete, and the non-refundable fee must be paid in advance. 
c) All applicable and incurred fees must be paid before the issuance of a renewed annual 
operating license.  
d) All such fees are based on a calendar year from February 1
st to January 31st. 
e) The District will email a renewal notice to the license holder on or before January 31st.  
f) Late fees will be assessed on March 1st, April 1st, and May 1st. Establishments not paying 
after 90 days will be closed according to this ordinance.  
g) The fee to replace a lost or destroyed License to Operate is $10.  
h) All fees must be paid online, by check or money order payable to the "Navajo 
County Public Health Services District."  
i) Returned checks will be assessed a late fee, a returned check fee of $25, and any legal fees 
assessed by the County Attorney’s office.  
 
Reg. 1-2-204 Transferability of a License or Permit 
a) An annual operating license or special event license is not transferable from person to person 
or place to place. 
b) A person who purchases a licensed establishment must apply for all required licenses or 
permits within five (5) business days of taking possession of the establishment or within five 
(5) business days after the close of escrow, whichever first occurs. Plan review and other 
applicable fees may be required. 
c) A person who changes the format, use, or operation of an existing licensed food establishment 
must apply for all required licenses or permits within 5 business days of the change. Plan 
review and other applicable fees may be required.  
 
Reg. 1-2-301 Denial of License or Permit 
a) The Health Officer may deny the issuance of a license or permit under the following 
circumstances: 
i) The applicant has not adequately demonstrated the knowledge or ability to operate the 
establishment in conformance with the requirements of the U.S. Food and Drug 
Administration (FDA) Food Code 2017, including the FDA Food Code Annexes; Arizona 
Department of Health Services regulations as set forth in Title 9, Chapter 8, Articles 1–13 
of the Arizona Administrative Code (A.A.C.); or the Navajo County Health Code. 
ii) If the application is incomplete or if supplementary information is needed for a complete 
plan, a commissary agreement, an operating plan, or a facility improvement plan. 
iii) If the license or permit fee is unpaid or declined for any reason. 
iv) If all applicable fees, including late fees, are unpaid or declined for any reason. 
b) The Health Officer may deny the issuance of a special event license under the following 
circumstances: 
i) If past inspections show a pattern of non-compliance with the Health Code or special event 
requirements. 
ii) Upon inspection, establishment is found to be unsanitary and not in compliance with the 
Health and Food Code.  
iii) If an application and related fees are repeatedly received in an untimely manner. 
iv) If payment for the special event license is declined for any reason. 
v) If all applicable fees have not been paid, including late fees.

<!-- page 165 -->
11 
 
c) Pursuant to A.R.S. § 41-1065, a person who has been denied a special event license shall be 
entitled to have a hearing on such denial upon filing within fifteen (15) days after receipt of 
notice of such refusal a written application for such hearing. 
d) A person who has been denied an annual operating license must: 
i) Close the establishment within 24 hours and, pursuant to A.R.S. § 41-1065, shall be 
entitled to have a hearing on such denial upon filing a written application for such hearing 
within fifteen (15) days after receipt of notice of such denial; or 
e) If an annual operating license, or a special event license is denied by the Department, then the 
person requesting the hearing bears the burden of establishing good cause why the annual 
operating license, or special event license should be issued by the Department.  
 
Reg. 1-2-302 Suspension of a License or Permit 
a) When a Health Inspector determines that conditions at a licensed premises or food 
establishment present an imminent health hazard requiring emergency action, the Health 
Officer or Health Inspector may order a summary suspension of the license or permit and may 
order suspension of the license or permit pending the outcome of proceedings for revocation 
of a license or other legal action. The summary suspension will incorporate the following 
findings: information supporting the emergency action, the pending proceedings for 
revocation, or other action as specified therein. Summary suspension of a license or permit 
will be for a period of not more than twenty-five (25) calendar days. 
b) Upon suspension of the license or permit, the licensed premises or food establishment will 
cease operations and the premises may be posted “closed” by the Health Inspector. If 
applicable a closure sign may be posted in a conspicuous place at the premises, clearly visible 
to the public, and will remain in place until removal is authorized by the Health Officer or 
Health Inspector or until the summary suspension expires. 
c) Upon suspension of the license or permit, the owner or owner’s representative thereof may 
immediately move to vacate the suspension order by making a written request for hearing to 
the Health Officer. The Hearing Officer will hear such motion within five (5) business days. In 
no event may a summary suspension remain in effect for more than twenty-five (25) calendar 
days. 
d) The Health Officer or Health Inspector may vacate the suspension of the license or permit 
upon a finding that conditions at the licensed premises or in the food establishment no longer 
present an imminent health hazard. 
 
Reg. 1-2-303 Revocation of a License or Permit 
a) The Health Officer may, after providing an opportunity for hearing, revoke a license under the 
following circumstances: 
i) Serious, repeated, or numerous violations of any of the requirements of the FDA Food 
Code 2017, including the FDA Food Code Annexes; Arizona Department of Health 
Services regulations as set forth in Title 9, Chapter 8, Article 1 – Article 13 of the A.A.C.; 
or the Navajo County Health Code. 
ii) An owner, owner’s representative, operator, manager, employee, or other person 
interfering with the Health Officer or Health Inspector in the performance of his/her 
duty(ies). A person who is convicted of a violation of this section is guilty of a Class 3 
misdemeanor. (Health Code Reg. 1-2-401). 
b) Prior to revocation, the Health Officer will provide written notification to the owner or 
owner’s representative of the licensed premises or food establishment the specific reason(s) 
the license is subject to being revoked and that the license will be revoked at the end of fifteen 
(15) days following service of such notice, unless a written request for a hearing is filed with

<!-- page 166 -->
12 
 
the Department by the owner or owner’s representative within fifteen (15) days after receipt of 
notice.  
c) If a request for a hearing is timely filed, the Hearing Officer will set a hearing pursuant to 
A.R.S. § 41-1061 and notice of the hearing shall be given at least twenty (20) days prior to the 
date set for the hearing. 
 
Reg. 1-2-304 Hearing Procedure 
a) Upon receiving an appeal and a request for a hearing regarding the denial, suspension, or 
revocation of a license or permit, the Health Officer will appoint a Hearing Officer to hold a 
hearing on the denial, suspension, or revocation. 
b) The Hearing Officer will be an impartial person who is trained in the law and is 
knowledgeable in the area of health law. The Hearing Officer may be a Navajo County 
employee. 
c) The Hearing Officer will notify the person requesting the hearing and the Department that a 
hearing will be held at a specified time and place pursuant to A.R.S. § 41-1061. 
d) Hearings will be conducted in accordance with the requirements of A.R.S. Title 41, Chapters 6 
and 10 and Title 9, Chapter 8, Articles 1–13 of the A.A.C. 
e) The person requesting the hearing and the Health Officer may appear on their own behalf or 
through legal counsel, may submit evidence in open hearing, and will have the right to 
confront and cross-examine witnesses. A corporation may appear only through a corporate 
officer or legal counsel. A company may only appear through a member or legal counsel. 
f) The Hearing Officer will conduct the hearing in a manner consistent with due process. 
Witnesses will be sworn, and their testimony will be recorded. The Hearing Officer will 
determine and consider relevant, probative, and material evidence and will disregard all 
irrelevant, immaterial, or unduly, repetitious evidence. The formal rules of evidence applicable 
to civil actions in superior court need not be followed. The standard of proof is preponderance 
of the evidence. The Hearing Officer may issue subpoenas, upon request, pursuant to A.R.S. 
Title 41, Chapters 6 and 10 and Title 9, Chapter 8, Articles 1–13 of the A.A.C. 
 
Reg. 1-2-305 Decision 
a) Within five (5) business days of the completion of a hearing for a license or permit denial or a 
license or permit revocation, the Hearing Officer will issue a written decision containing 
his/her findings of facts and conclusions of law that form the basis of the Hearing Officer’s 
decision.  
b) The decision of the Hearing Officer will become effective immediately upon service of notice 
as outlined in Health Code Reg. 1-2-310. If the decision of the Hearing Officer is to revoke a 
license, the owner or the owner’s representative must close the premises within twenty-four 
(24) hours of notification. 
c) At its next scheduled meeting following the issuance of the Hearing Officer’s decision, the 
Board of Health will ratify the Hearing Officer’s decision, unless a timely request for 
rehearing or reconsideration is submitted to the Health Officer pursuant to Health Code Reg. 
1-2-306 and A.R.S. § 36-183.04. 
 
Reg. 1-2-306 Request for Rehearing or Reconsideration 
a) A party may file a request for rehearing, review, or reconsideration through the Department 
with the Board of Health within thirty (30) days after service of the final administrative 
decision pursuant to A.R.S. § 41-1092.09. 
b) The request for rehearing, review, or reconsideration may be based on any of the following 
causes which materially affected the party’s rights:

<!-- page 167 -->
13 
 
i) Irregularity in the proceedings or hearing whereby the aggrieved party was deprived of a 
fair hearing; 
ii) Misconduct of a party, the Health Officer, or the Hearing Officer; 
iii) Newly discovered material evidence which, with reasonable diligence, could not have 
been discovered and produced at the hearing; 
iv) That the decision is the result of prejudice; or 
v) That the decision is not justified by the evidence or is contrary to law. 
c) At its next scheduled meeting following receipt of a request for rehearing, review, or 
reconsideration, the Board of Health will deny the request, grant a hearing de novo, or issue a 
reconsidered decision based on the evidence of record. The Board of Health’s denial, decision 
after a hearing de novo, or reconsidered decision will be the final decision of the Department. 
Notice of that denial, decision, or reconsidered decision will be personally delivered or mailed 
by certified mail to the party(ies). 
 
Reg. 1-2-307 Time 
a) The computation of any time limits concerning the hearing process are calculated as provided 
for by Arizona statutes and the Arizona Rules of Civil Procedure. 
b) The time limits in these rules may be enlarged or reduced by agreement of the parties. The 
Hearing Officer may grant a continuance of a hearing by stipulation of the parties or when the 
interests of justice so require. 
 
Reg. 1-2-308 Emergency Action 
a) If an immediate and substantial public health danger exists, the Health Officer’s decision will 
be final, pending the request for hearing by the person whom the action adversely affects. If 
the request for hearing is filed in such a case, the appointment of a Hearing Officer and the 
request for hearing must occur within a reasonable time, and no later than the time limits 
described by Arizona law or the Health Code. 
b) In the case of suspension of a license, the Hearing Officer will set a hearing on a motion to 
vacate the suspension within five (5) working days of receiving notice. At the conclusion of 
the hearing, the Hearing Officer will issue a final decision on behalf of and with the authority 
of the Board of Health. The Hearing Officer may take the matter under advisement for up to 
twenty-four (24) hours. A party dissatisfied with the decision of the Hearing Officer may file a 
request for rehearing or reconsideration with the Board of Health pursuant to Health Code 
Reg. 1-2-306 “Request for Rehearing or Reconsideration.” 
 
Reg. 1-2-309 Posting of Notice(s) of Violation  
a) The Health Officer or Health Inspector may, for purposes of notification to the public, post a 
sign at the premises describing the nature of any violations or legal action pending against said 
premises, including notification of the license suspension or license revocation proceedings.  
 
 
Reg. 1-2-310 Service of Notices 
a) A notice provided for in the Health Code for enforcement and appeals is properly served when 
it is hand delivered to the owner or owner’s representative, or when it is sent by registered or 
certified mail, return receipt requested, to the last known address of the owner or owner’s 
representative and/or last known email. A copy of the notice will be filed in the records of the 
Department. The notice must comply with all legal requirements.

<!-- page 168 -->
14 
 
Reg. 1-2-401 Penalties 
a) A person who violates the Navajo County Health Code; FDA Food Code 2017, including the 
FDA Food Code Annexes; or A.A.C. Title 9, Chapter 8, Articles 1 - 13 may have the 
establishment’s license or permit suspended or revoked pursuant to the Health Code. 
b) A person who violates Arizona law; the Health Code; the FDA Food Code 2017, including the 
FDA Food Code Annexes; A.A.C. Title 9, Chapter 8, Articles 1 – 13; a sanitary ordinance; a 
regulation or order; or an order issued pursuant to A.R.S. § 36602 is guilty of a Class 3 
misdemeanor if the person holds a valid permit or license, or guilty of a Class 2 misdemeanor 
if the person does not hold a valid permit or license. [A.R.S. § 36-183.07]. 
c) A person is guilty of a Class 3 misdemeanor if a person interferes in the performance of the 
duty of the Health Officer or Health Inspector. [Health Code Reg. 1-2-303(a)(ii); A.R.S. § 36-
183.07]. 
d) In addition to a license or permit fee, a late fee (Table 1) will be assessed in increments for a 
license renewed more than thirty (30), sixty (60) and ninety (90) days after the expiration of 
the license or permit. 
e) An annual operating license will be classified as out of business for failure of the owner or 
owner's representative to renew the annual operating license within ninety (90) days after the 
license expires, and the owner or owner’s representative will be operating without a license or 
permit. A person who is convicted for failure to obtain or retain a license or permit is guilty of 
a Class 2 misdemeanor. 
f) A compliance fee that is set by the Board of Supervisors will be assessed for program 
compliance re-inspections and repeat re-inspections. 
g) The Health officer or Health Inspector may document on the Inspection the notice of non-
payment as a Priority Violation and mark the inspection as “Needs Improvement”.  
h) The Department will set late fees for applications for a special event license if an application 
for a special event license is received less than 21 days before the event. 
i) An investigation fee that is set by the Board of Supervisors will be assessed if discovery is 
made of a facility constructing, remodeling, or operating without prior Department approval. 
j) Penalties may be assessed up to and including revocation of a license if payment of a 
sanctioned or incurred fee has not been paid within ninety (90) days of invoice or application 
for a license, whichever is sooner. The Director may order the immediate closure of any 
facility for which a required license has not been obtained and may pursue all available legal 
remedies to enforce such order. The County Attorney is hereby authorized to assist 
the Director in the enforcement of this ordinance. 
 
Reg. 1-2-501 Embargoed Food  
a) The Department, having reason to suspect that any food is from an unapproved source, 
unwholesome, spoiled or otherwise is unsafe or unfit for human consumption, may forthwith 
label or seal such food as “withheld” or “embargoed.” Such labeled or sealed food must be set 
apart from other food and not used, sold, given away, or discarded until the Department has 
 
had reasonable opportunity to examine the food. After examination, the Department may 
remove the label or seal, may direct the owner, owner’s representative, or person in charge of 
the food to denature, remove, or destroy such food or to bring it into compliance with the 
requirements of the Health Code. A person who is convicted of a violation hereunder is guilty 
of a Class 2 misdemeanor.

<!-- page 169 -->
15 
 
Reg. 1-2-502 Condemned Equipment 
a) If after examination, the Department determines that a food utensil or food equipment is worn, 
defective, unsanitary, or otherwise prejudicial to health, such utensil or equipment may be 
labeled “condemned” by the Department and the utensil or equipment so labeled may not 
thereafter be used for food storage, preparation, handling, or serving. The Department may 
direct the owner or owner’s representative to bring the condemned utensil or equipment into 
compliance with the requirements of the Health Code, remove it from the food establishment, 
or replace it with approved units. A person who is convicted of a violation hereunder is guilty 
of a Class 3 misdemeanor. 
 
Reg. 1-2-503 Removal of Seal  
a) A “withheld,” “embargoed” or “condemned” label, tag or seal, having once been affixed by 
the Department to food or equipment, must be removed only by the Department except as 
otherwise provided by law. A person who is convicted of a violation hereunder is guilty of a 
Class 2 misdemeanor. 
 
 
Chapter 3 
 
Reg. 1-3-101 Plan Review—Building Plan 
a) The establishments listed below may require plan review and approval by the Department 
before construction or remodeling is started. Plan review will be in accordance with the FDA 
Food Code 2017, including the FDA Food Code Annexes; Arizona Department of Health 
Services regulations as set forth in Title 9, Chapter 8, Articles 1 – 13 of the A.A.C.; and the 
Navajo County Health Code. 
i) Food Establishments 
ii) Bottled Water 
iii) Children’s Camps 
iv) Trailer Coach Parks – service buildings, toilet facilities, community kitchens 
v) Campgrounds 
vi) Schools 
vii) Hotels, Motels and Tourist Courts 
b) The Department may accept the plan review and approval of plans as recommended by the 
Arizona Department of Environmental Quality or the Arizona Department of Health Services. 
c) The Department is not obligated to accept a plan review and approval of plans issued by any 
jurisdiction. 
d) Within thirty (30) business days of the Department receiving the plans, the Department will 
send a notice advising the applicant of the status of the plan review. 
e) The Department time frames for completion of regulatory reviews are set forth by the Arizona 
Department of Health Services (A.A.C. Title 9, Chapter 8, Article 1, Table 1). 
f) The Department will: 
i) Issue a written approval of the plans; 
 
ii) Issue a written statement requesting additional information; or 
iii) Issue a written denial of the submitted plans and reasons for the denial. 
g) Plans must be approved when the minimum requirements of the Department are satisfied. 
h) The applicant will receive a written notice of approval of the plans. 
i) Expedited plans are assessed a fee (Table 1).

<!-- page 170 -->
16 
 
Reg. 1-3-102 Plan Review—Operation Plan (Food Safety Plan) 
a) New Establishments and Operations  
i) The Health Inspector may require a meeting with the owner or owner’s representative at 
least thirty (30) days before opening for business to review the establishment’s operating 
plan.  
ii) The operating plan must be in writing and must describe the following:  
(1) The person or position responsible for the overall operation of the establishment;  
(2) Identified public health hazards and how the hazards will be controlled or managed;  
(3) The person or position responsible for controlling or managing the public health 
hazard;  
(4) What public health hazard test(s) or measurement(s) will be taken, by whom and 
when;  
(5) Legal requirements concerning the public health hazard; and  
(6) Corrective action that will be taken, by whom and when, if the legal requirements are 
not satisfied.  
iii) Any additional information requested by the Health Inspector to complete the operational 
plan review must be submitted in writing to the Department.  
iv) All written information submitted by the license applicant must be legible.  
b) The Health Officer may waive or modify the requirements of Health Code Reg. 1-3-
102(a)(ii).  
c) A new owner of an existing food establishment must comply with Health Code Reg. 1-3-102.  
d) A person applying for an annual operating license must comply with Health Code Reg. 1-3-
102.  
e) The approved operating plan must be instituted on or before the day the food establishment 
opens for business. For change of ownership of a food establishment, this requirement may be 
extended; however, no extension will be granted past any ninety (90) day provisional permit.  
f) Onsite documentation that the operating plan is adhered to on a daily basis must be available 
for the Health Inspector’s review.  
g) Before initiating any changes to the operating plan, the proposed changes must be submitted to 
the Health Inspector, in writing, for review and approval.  
h) Failure to prepare and maintain an approved operating plan under the Health Code is a priority 
foundation item. 
 
Reg. 1-3-103 Approval 
a) Approval to begin construction will be issued when the Department is satisfied that the 
proposed facility meets the minimum requirements of the Department.  
 
Reg. 1-3-104 Non-approval to Construct 
a) The Department may refuse to issue an approval to construct under the following 
circumstances:   
i) Failure of the applicant to submit the plan review fee;  
ii) Lack of information on the plan review application; or  
iii) Failure of the applicant to submit additional information requested by the Health 
Inspector.  
 
Reg. 1-3-105  Denial of a License or Permit  
a) The Department may deny an application for a license under the following circumstances:   
i) Failure of the applicant to pay in full the permit fee;  
ii) Failure of the applicant to complete the application; or

<!-- page 171 -->
17 
 
iii) Failure of the owner or owner’s representative to submit an food safety plan, food manager 
certification or enrollment letter, or information as requested by the Health Inspector.  
 
Reg. 1-3-106 Grease Trap and Grease Interceptor 
a) This subsection applies to a food establishment that generates grease in the wastewater.   
i) The size and need of a grease trap or grease interceptor will be determined by the 
appropriate jurisdiction.  
ii) The location of an inside grease trap must be approved by the appropriate jurisdiction. If 
the appropriate jurisdiction is the Department, then the Department will consider the 
following.  
(1) Preferred location is outside of the building.  
(2) If a grease trap needs to be installed inside of the food establishment, it must not be 
located in the food preparation or food service areas.  
(3) Based on approval of the Health Inspector, a grease trap may be installed in the 
dishwashing area and should be flush with the floor.  
iii) A grease trap or grease interceptor must be located and installed in accordance with the 
requirements of the appropriate jurisdiction.  
 
 
Chapter 4 – Food, Food Products, Food Establishments 
 
Reg. 1-4-101 Cider, Juice, and Beverage Production 
a) A cider, juice, or beverage food establishment must apply for a license to operate pursuant to 
Health Code Reg. 1-2-201 through 1-2-204.  
b) Cider, juice, and beverage products must comply with all provisions of the FDA Food Code 
2017, including the FDA Food Code Annexes; A.A.C. Title 9, Chapter 8, Article 1; and the 
Health Code.  
c) Fruit must be in sound condition, free from spoilage, filth, or other contamination and must be 
safe for human consumption. Insect infested, decayed, damaged, or rotten fruit must not be 
used.  
d) All fruit must be thoroughly cleaned and inspected before processing.  
e) Cleaned fruit for processing must be stored in clean, sanitized, food-grade containers and held 
at or below 41°F. Fruit for processing must be stored in such a manner that will prevent 
contamination from any source, including flies and other insects, rodents, birds, and other 
vermin.  
f) Cloths and filters used in processing must be specifically designed for the purpose for which 
they are used.  
g) Cloths, unless disposable, must be laundered, sanitized, and dried after processing and before 
being reused.  
h) Equipment or machine tubing must be cleaned, sanitized, and air-dried as set forth in the FDA 
Food Code 2017, including the FDA Food Code Annexes.  
i) The following processing records must be kept daily:  
i) Number of bottled beverages produced;  
ii) Amount and type of fruit processed;   
iii) Origin of fruit and ingredients used; and  
iv) Production code and date.  
j) Bottled products must only be sold in sanitized new containers with new caps.  
k) The bottled product must be rapidly cooled to 41°F or below and then maintained at or below 
41° F.

<!-- page 172 -->
18 
 
l) All labeling requirements must be satisfied according to the FDA Food Code 2017, including 
the FDA Food Code Annexes; A.A.C. Title 9, Chapter 8, Article 1; and the Health Code.  
m) Plans must be submitted to the Department for review and approval before starting 
construction or remodeling pursuant to the Health Code.  
n) Bottling must be done in an enclosed and approved area that has smooth, easily cleanable, 
light colored walls and ceiling, and a smooth, easily cleanable non-absorbent floor.  
o) Fruit pressing must be done in an enclosed and approved area.  
p) A cider, juice, and beverage producer must submit a written proposal for approval to the 
Department which includes the following information:  
i) A complete list of food product(s) to be made;  
ii) A list of the ingredients and all steps that are used to prepare the food product;  
iii) A list of all food equipment that will be used;  
iv) A list of the types of packaging to be used and how food product will be packaged; and  
v) A sample copy of all labeling to be used. All required labeling must be according to the 
FDA Food Code 2017, including the FDA Food Code Annexes; A.A.C. Title 9, Chapter 8, 
Article 1; and the Health Code.  
q) Food product testing may be required by the Department to ensure the safety and shelf 
stability of the food products as they are packaged. The food processor will be responsible for 
arranging all the testing and providing the test results to the Department.  
r) All processors are required to abide by Federal, State, and County laws, regulations, and 
requirements pertaining to their food and beverage product.  
 
 
Reg. 1-4-102 Mobile Food Requirements 
a) A mobile food establishment must apply for a license or permit to operate pursuant to Health 
Code Reg. 1-2-201 through 1-2-204 and A.R.S. § 11-269.20.  
b) A mobile food establishment must comply with the FDA Food Code 2017, including the FDA 
Food Code Annexes; A.R.S. § 11-269.20; A.A.C. Title 9, Chapter 8, Article 1; and the Health 
Code.  
c) A mobile food establishment must operate from an approved commissary kitchen and report to 
the commissary kitchen as often as necessary for food preparation, required supplies, cleaning 
and sanitizing of food equipment, and all servicing operations that are required to be done at 
or in the commissary.  
i) The commissary will be limited to the number of mobile food establishments it can 
effectively handle. The Department will set guidelines for setting limitations to the number 
of mobile food establishments that can report to a commissary, including, but not limited 
to, criteria for the menu, the volume of food being prepared or stored, the physical size of 
the mobile food establishment, or the capacity of the equipment at the commissary and in 
the mobile food establishment.  
ii) The commissary must maintain reports on the premises as to the mobile food 
establishment’s usage of the commissary, including, but not limited to, dates, arrival times,  
and departure times. A commissary must present reports to the Department or Health 
Inspector upon request. Failure to maintain the required reports or falsification of the 
required reports will be grounds for revoking permission for the food establishment to act 
as a commissary.  
iii) A commissary must provide outside access to potable water for the filling of the 
freshwater tanks in the mobile food establishment and outside dumping station for the 
dumping of grey liquid wastewater from the mobile food establishment. A safe alternative

<!-- page 173 -->
19 
 
method may be approved for providing potable water for filling of freshwater tanks or 
dumping of grey water waste.  
d) All mobile food establishments must be identified on three (3) sides of the unit specifying with 
a minimum of 4” lettering the business name and contact information.  
e) All mobile food establishments will maintain a location of operation log sheet reflecting 
routine stops or approximate areas of operation (e.g., cross streets, construction site, etc.). Log 
sheets must also include approximate times of arrival and departure for each location of 
operation. A mobile food unit must present the required location of operation log sheet upon 
request of the Health Officer or Health Inspector. Failure to maintain the operation log sheet or 
falsification of the required location of the operation log sheet may be grounds for revocation 
of a license or permit.  
 
Reg. 1-4-103 Food Delivery and Catering 
a) A caterer or food caterer must comply with the FDA Food Code 2017, including the FDA 
Food Code Annexes; A.A.C. Title 9, Chapter 8, Article 1; and the Health Code. 
b) A caterer must apply for a license or permit to operate pursuant to Health Code Reg. 1- 2-201 
through 1-2-204.  
c) The Department will include any limitations or conditions for operation of the license or permit.  
d) A licensed food establishment may submit a written request to add “Catering” to the existing 
license.  
i) The written request will be reviewed by the Department;  
ii) The information requested by the Department must be submitted; and  
iii) If approved, the license limitations and conditions will be added to the license.  
e) A mobile food establishment that transports and solicits sales of prepackaged time/temperature 
control for safety food to one or more location, sites, or businesses during a working day must 
be licensed as a mobile food establishment.  
f) A mobile food establishment that delivers time/temperature control for safety food or prepares 
food at one or more locations, sites, or businesses during a working day must be licensed as a 
mobile food establishment.  
g) A mobile food establishment owned and used exclusively by an approved food establishment 
for catering (pursuant to Health Code Reg. 1-4-103(a-j) or at a single location at the 
customer’s request for his/her guests may be covered under the food establishment’s annual 
operating license.  
h) Food requiring only limited preparation or cooking such as frankfurters, pre-formed meat 
patties, cuts of meat, poultry parts, or other food items as specified and approved by the 
Department that must be prepared for the customer at the delivery location.  
i) Food cooked at the delivery location must be served directly from the master container or grill 
to the customer. Cooked food must not be maintained or stored at the delivery location for 
more than four (4) hours.  
j) Food to be prepared or cooked for consumption at an outdoor delivery location must be 
prepared, cooked, packaged, and protected as required by the Department. 
 
Reg. 1-4-104 Food Processor 
a) A food processor must apply for a license or permit to operate pursuant to Health Code Reg. 
1-2-201 through 1-2-204.  
b) A food processor must comply with the FDA Food Code 2017, including the FDA Food Code 
Annexes; A.A.C. Title 9, Chapter 8, Article 1; and the Health Code.  
c) All food processors must operate out of and conduct all food processing at an approved 
commissary or its licensed fixed food establishment.

<!-- page 174 -->
20 
 
d) A food processor operating out of an approved commissary must perform all operations of the 
food preparation, cleaning and sanitizing of equipment and utensils, packaging of food items, 
storage of equipment and all supplies, as well as anything that deals with processing of the 
food item.  
i) The commissary will be limited to the number of processors it can effectively handle.  
ii) The Department will provide guidelines setting limitations for the number of processors 
that can report to a commissary, including, but not limited to, criteria concerning the menu, 
the volume of food being prepared or stored, and the capacity of the equipment at the 
commissary or in the processor’s facility.   
e) A food processor must submit a written proposal to the Department for approval as to:  
i) A complete list of food product(s) to be made;  
ii) A list of the ingredients and all steps that are used to prepare the food product;  
iii) A list of all food equipment that will be used;  
iv) A list of the types of packaging to be used and how the food product will be packaged; 
and  
v) A sample copy of all labeling to be used. All required labeling must be in accordance with 
the FDA Food Code 2017, including the FDA Food Code Annexes; A.A.C. Title 9, 
Chapter 8, Article 1; and the Health Code.  
f) Food product testing may be required by the Department to ensure the safety and shelf 
stability of the food products. The food processor will be responsible for arranging all the 
testing and providing the test results to the Department.  
g) All processors are required to abide by Federal, State, and County laws, regulations, and 
requirements pertaining to their food product.  
 
 
Chapter 5--Food Workers 
 
Reg. 1-5-101 Food Worker Certificate 
a) Each person operating or applying for a license to operate a food establishment must require 
each food worker to obtain a food worker certificate from the Department.  
b) A food worker certificate must be obtained by each food worker at a food establishment 
within thirty (30) days after the start of employment. The cost of the food worker certificate 
will be established by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors and will be listed in Table 1.   
c) The Department will issue a food worker certificate when the applicant has successfully 
completed a Department approved food worker certification course, which may include an 
Accredited Program such as the American National Standards Institute and the Conference for 
Food Protection.  
d) Failure to obtain or maintain a food worker certificate under the Health Code is a core item.  
 
Reg. 1-5-102 Manager Certification 
a) All food establishments that store, prepare, package, serve, vend, or otherwise provide     
time/temperature control for safety food or food for human consumption must employ at least 
one (1) certified manager that is present and available during operational hours.   
b) The person in charge on-site must be a certified manager.  
c) A food establishment with fewer than ten (10) employees may satisfy Health Code Reg. 1-5-
102(a) by having a written Department-approved food safety plan in place and provided that a 
separate certified manager for each food establishment is available for a minimum of eight (8) 
hours each day that the food establishment is in operation or open for business.  
d) Manager Certification must be current and valid.

<!-- page 175 -->
21 
e) Failure to obtain or maintain a manager certification under the Health Code is a priority
foundation item.
f) Food Establishments exempt from manager certification requirements are as follows:
i) Temporary food establishments:
(1) A temporary food establishment operated by a service organization;
(2) A temporary food establishment whose profits entirely go to a service organization; or
(3) A temporary food establishment that operates for less than four (4) hours per event,
including time involved to prepare food for cooking or service.
ii) Limited food operation establishments that sell only commercially prepackaged food.
iii) An exempt food establishment must have at least one person who has a valid Food Worker
Certificate at the premises at all times overseeing food preparation and service.
R
eg. 1-5-103 Display of Food Worker Certificate and Manager Certification 
a) In every food establishment the manager certification and food worker certificate for each
person employed therein and engaged in food handling must be prominently posted where it
can be seen and inspected by the Health Inspector.
C
hapter 6—No n-Food Establishments 
1. PUBLIC AND SEMIPUBLIC BATHING PLACES: LICENSE TO OPERATE
Each Public or Semipublic Bathing Place that is subject to regulation under Title 9, Chapter 
8, Article 8 of the Arizona Administrative Code shall obtain and post in public view a current 
License to Operate from the District. The issuance of a License to Operate and each 
renewal thereof shall be conditioned upon compliance with all applicable requirements of 
Title 9, Chapter  8, Article 8 of the Arizona Administrative Code, as  determined by  the 
District. 
2. WATER HAULERS: LICENSE TO OPERATE
Each Water Hauler that is subject to regulation under Title 18, Chapter 4, Article 1, R18 - 
4-214 of the Arizona Administrative Code shall obtain and keep in the primary transport
vehicle a current License to Operate from the District. The issuance of a License t o
Operate and each renewal thereof shall be conditioned upon compliance with all
applicable requirements of Title 18, Chapter 4, Article 1, R18-4- 214 of the Arizon a
Administrative Code, as determined by the District.
3. SEPTIC HAULERS: LICENSE TO OPERATE
Each Septic Hauler that is subject to regulation under Title 18, Chapter 13, Article 11 of the 
Arizona Administrative Code shall obtain and keep in the primary transport vehicle a 
current License to Operate from the District.  The issuance of a License to Operate and 
each renewal thereof shall be conditioned upon compliance with all applicable 
requirements of Title 18, Chapter 13, Article 11 of the Arizona Administrative Code, as 
determined by the District. 
SO, ORDAINED by the Board of Directors of the Navajo County Public Health Services 
District.

Board of Supervisors Regular 7. a. 2.        
Meeting Date: 10/25/2022  
Title: Intergovernmental Agreement between Navajo County and City of Winslow
Submitted For: Janelle Linn, Health Director 
Submitted By: Rhonda Krouse, Code Enforcement Division Manager 
Department: NC Public Health Services District
Presented By: Rhonda Krouse
Motion before the Board:
Consideration for approval of an Intergovernmental Agreement for Animal Impoundment
Services between Navajo County Public Health Services District and the City of Winslow.  
Background:
The City of Winslow Animal Care & Control department agrees to assist the NC Public Health
Services District Animal Care & Control Department by allowing for kennel space for the
NCPHSD Animal Care & Control Program to utilize for their animal shelter for hold and
quarantine needs. The Animal Care & Control Program agrees to pay the City of Winslow a flat
fee of $75.00 per animal for a maximum of 10 days.  We request approval of the contract by the
board.
Recommendation: 
Board of Supervisors approval of the IGA.

Winslow Contract Updated

1 
 
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
Intergovernmental Agreement  
for  
Animal Impoundment Services  
between  
Navajo County and the City of Winslow 
 
This Intergovernmental Agreement is made as of October 1st, 2022, (the “Effective Date") by 
and between the CITY OF WINSLOW an Arizona municipal corporation, (“CITY"), and 
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH, a political subdivision of the State of Arizona 
("COUNTY"): 
 
I. RECITALS 
 
1. The parties are authorized pursuant to A.R.S. § 11-952 and by ordinance to 
perform animal control activities within their respective jurisdictions. 
2. The CITY owns and operates an Animal Control Center at 548 Barrigan Rd in 
Winslow for its own animal control activities. 
3. The CITY is willing to temporarily hold animals from the COUNTY at 
its Animal Control Center upon the terms and conditions set forth 
herein. 
 
II. AGREEMENT 
 
1. The Recitals above are incorporated into this Agreement as set forth in full. 
2. This Agreement shall remain in effect from the Effective Date until terminated as 
set forth in paragraph 7 below. 
3. CITY agrees to assist COUNTY by temporary holding of animals for 
impoundment by seizures throughout the county and/or under quarantine for a 
maximum of 10 days if space if available. If the animal is still with the CITY after 
10 days, the COUNTY will take possession of the animal. 
4. For each dog accepted for impoundment at City Animal Control Center, 
COUNTY agrees to pay to CITY a flat fee of $75.00 for animals brought in only 
by the COUNTY Animal Care & Control Officers, or by CITY Animal Control 
officers if animal was seized within County limits.  This fee shall be inclusive of 
all services typically provided by the CITY in connection with dogs impounded 
at the Animal Control Center, including feeding and euthanasia, If necessary. 
This fee may be amended from time to time by mutual agreement of the CITY 
and the COUNTY, any such amendments shall be in writing and approved by 
the COUNTY’s Board of Supervisors and attached hereto. 
A. The COUNTY understands that the flat fee per dog does not include 
medical treatment for which the services of a veterinarian are required.  If 
such services are required, the COUNTY will be notified before these 
services and may decide against such services.  If services are agreed the

Board of Supervisors Regular 8. a.        
Meeting Date: 10/25/2022  
Title: Application for Historic Preservation Heritage Funds for Pinedale Elementary School
Restoration
Submitted By: Kenichi Maruyama, Strategic Initiatives Manager
Department: Administrative Services Division: Finance
Presented By: Bret Starns/Ken Maruyama
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of approving an application for an FY 2023 Historic Preservation Heritage Fund
grant assistance to request $100,790 for Pinedale Elementary School Restoration (Phase I) with
local match of $67,194, and authorizing the Facilities Director to submit the application and
manage the project in compliance with the grant's terms and conditions. 
Background:
Pinedale Elementary School was listed on the National Register of Historic Places March 8, 2002.
The structure was designed in 1939, with construction completed in 1942. The structure is wood
frame and houses a small multipurpose gym, two classrooms and a partial basement. The Pinedale
School is culturally significant in the development of the Pinedale community. It is located
directly due south from Pinedale’s other defining feature, the historic covered bridge that leads in
the town.  
 
Navajo County hired Johnson Walzer Associates (JWA) Architects, a consultant approved
by the State Historic Preservation Office (SHPO), to examine the county-owned building.
According to the assessment report, the exterior of this building needs works at all walls
and the roof. These elements have lost their watertightness. Without immediate assistance,
these elements will continue to decay, further damaging this historic structure.
 
Based on JWA Architects’ recommendations, the total project cost, including 10% A/E
design, is estimated at $308,530. Navajo County Administration plans to conduct the
project by phase: Phase I ($167,984 for roofing, drainage, and entrance) and Phase II
($140,546 for stucco and windows).
The State's Historic Preservation Heritage Fund Program provides funds for the purpose of
preserving historic and prehistoric resources. In order to obtain the funding for the Phase I
project, staff completed a pre-application process and obtained access to application
documents from SHPO. The application deadline is November 1, 2022.  
Recommendation: 
Staff recommend approval.

<!-- page 191 -->
Staff recommend approval. 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2023
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $67,194
Fiscal Impact:
Navajo County plans to request $100,790 from FY 2023 Historic Preservation Heritage Fund grant
assistance program for Pinedale Elementary School Restoration (Phase I) with local match of
$67,194.

Summarize the project proposal including scope and nature of what is to be accomplished. What will be accomplished with the funds that you will receive?
This field is limited to 1,750 characters.
Brief Description of Project:* Navajo County proposes restoring a nationally registered historic building, Pinedale Elementary School, with support from Arizona State Historic Preservation Office (SHPO).  
The building was listed on the National Register of Historic Places March 8, 2002. The structure was designed in 1939, with construction completed in 1942. The structure is wood frame and houses a small multipurpose gym, two classrooms and a partial basement. The Pinedale School is culturally significant in the development of the Pinedale community. It is located directly due south from Pinedale?s other defining feature, the historic covered bridge that leads in the town. 
To preserve the building, Navajo County hired Johnson Walzer Associates (JWA) Architects, listed as a SHPO approved consultant, to examine the building. According to the assessment report, the exterior of this building needs works at all walls and the roof. These elements have lost their watertightness. Without immediate assistance, these elements will continue to decay, further damaging this historic structure. The needs are in four general areas: 1) Exterior Stucco, 2) Wood Windows, 3) Roof & Roof Edge, and 4) Building Perimeter Grading.  
Based on JWA Architects? recommendations, the total project cost, including 25% design, permit, and construction costs, is estimated at $350,603, for which Navajo County requests $210,362 to SHPO Heritage Fund Grant Program with local match of $140,241. As a property owner, the County is committed to completing the proposed project in compliance with the grant?s terms and conditions.

RESOLUTION ____-22 
 
APPROVING THE APPLICATION FOR HISTORIC PRESERVATION HERITAGE 
FUNDS FOR PINEDALE ELEMENTARY SCHOOL RESTORATION (PHASE I) 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Legislature under A.R.S. § 41-503 has authorized the establishment of the 
Historic Preservation Heritage Fund Program providing funds to the State of Arizona and other 
eligible applicants for the purpose of preserving historic and prehistoric resources; and  
 
WHEREAS, the Arizona State Parks Board (BOARD) is responsible for the administration of the 
program within the State, setting up necessary rules and procedures governing application under the 
program; and 
 
WHEREAS, said adopted procedures established by the BOARD require the applicant to certify by 
resolution the approval of applications, signature authorization, the availability of local matching 
funds, and authorization to sign a Participant Agreement with the BOARD; and 
 
WHEREAS, the proposed project must be consistent with the Arizona Historic Preservation Plan; 
and 
 
WHEREAS, Pinedale Elementary School was listed on the National Register of Historic Places 
March 8, 2002. The structure was designed in 1939, with construction completed in 1942. The 
structure is wood frame and houses a small multipurpose gym, two classrooms and a partial 
basement. The Pinedale  School is culturally significant in the development of the Pinedale 
community. It is located directly due south from Pinedale’s other defining feature, the historic 
covered bridge that leads in the town; and 
 
WHEREAS, Navajo County hired Jo hnson Walzer Associates (JWA) Architects, a consultant 
approved by the State Historic Preservation Office (SHPO), to examine the county-owned building. 
According to the assessment report, the exterior of this building needs works at all walls and the 
roof. These elements have lost their watertightness. Without immediate assistance, these elements 
will continue to decay, further damaging this historic structure; and 
 
WHEREAS, based on JWA Architects’ recommendations, the total project cost, including 10% 
A/E design, is estimated at $308,530. Navajo County Administration plans to conduct the project by 
phase: Phase I ($167,984 for roofing, drainage, and entrance) and Phase II ($140,546 for stucco and 
windows).

by the BOARD as a part of the application process; and 
4. Certifies that Navajo County has matching funds in the amount of $67,194 from general

Board of Supervisors Regular 9. a.        
Meeting Date: 10/25/2022  
Title: Task Agreement No. 29
Submitted For: Bill Bess, County Engineer/Assistant Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Miles Begay, Engineering Tribal Trans Manager
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Bill Bess
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of Task Agreement No. 29 to the Intergovernmental Agreement between the
Bureau of Indian Affairs-Navajo Region and Navajo County that authorizes funding assistance for
transmission repairs on an inventoried BIA motor grader that has been utilized by County
equipment operator. 
Background:
This agreement is an extension on time for expired Task Agreement No. 28, which was created
from a request received from BIA-Chinle Agency to assist them in fixing a motor grader that was
utilized by Navajo County's District 1 operator. The reason of no action taken on the expired
agreement was due to Bureau of Indian Affair's financial department unable to get their portion
paid for on the cost sharing. This motor grader provided road maintenance service within the
Black Mesa Region of District 1, unfortunately with the wear and tear on these equipments caused
a transmission failure which will need some extensive work to repair. BIA received a new cost
estimate of $45k to complete repairs. Navajo County would like to continue working
cooperatively with BIA-Chinle Agency in repairing and staffing this equipment in preparation of
the upcoming winter season.     
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval of the attached Task Agreement between Bureau of Indian
Affairs-Navajo Region and Navajo County. 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2023
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $45,000
Fiscal Impact:
$45,000 will be allocated from District 1 Navajo Nation HURF funds.

Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors, by motion duly passed at the Board’s regular 
meeting on October 25, 2022, has approved the expenditure of up to $45,000 in (X) 
Special Project Road Funds, or ( ) Highway User Revenue Funds to be used for the 
funding of repairing a motor grader request of Chinle BIA office, in the continued 
support of repairing road maintenance activities on BIA public roads, which are located 
on the Navajo Indian Reservation within BIA Chinle Agency, in Navajo County. The 
funds shall be used to support maintenance repairs or replacement parts on BIA heavy 
equipment that is being utilized to maintain routes within the Navajo Reservation, within 
Navajo County’s Supervisorial District No. 1.  
The BIA warrants and represents that the materials purchased with the Allocated Funds 
will be used primarily for maintenance activities on roads within Navajo County, which 
are open to the public and are eligible for the expenditure of HURF monies under the 
Arizona Constitution and related Arizona Revised Statutes. The BIA to fully cooperate in 
any audit of the Allocated Funds by the Arizona Auditor General. 
For any material acquired in whole or in part with the Allocated Funds, the acquiring 
party shall be solely responsible for all expenses, including without limitation preventive 
maintenance, repairs and insurance. The County shall have no responsibility or liability 
whatsoever in regard to such material. The BIA shall be responsible for identifying, 
prioritizing, scheduling and performing all maintenance activities for which the Allocated 
Funds are used in whole or in part. It is understood that the County’s sole obligation 
hereunder is to make payment of the Allocated Funds. 
Any Allocated Funds that are unused as of June 30, 2023 shall no longer be available 
for distribution pursuant to this Memorandum of Agreement. The Allocated Funds under 
this agreement shall be paid by Navajo County directly to the invoicing party upon the 
County’s receipt of invoices or billing statement that show that the items have been 
purchased and approved by the BIA. The total of all invoices shall not exceed the 
approved amount from this agreement.

Board of Supervisors Regular 9. b.        
Meeting Date: 10/25/2022  
Title: Woody’s RV Park and Storage 
Submitted For: Mischa Larisch, PW Deputy Director of P & Z 
Submitted By: Berrin Nejad, Community Development Director
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Zoning
Presented By: Berrin Nejad
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration of an adoption of Resolution Number ___-2022
approving/denying a Special Use Permit request for Woody’s RV Park and Storage with 25
overnight RV Camping and RV Storage area, APN # 206-02-012C, T12N, R17E, S30.
Background:
The applicant, Steve Wood, is requesting approval of a Special Use Permit for an RV Park and
storage facility. The property is zoned C-R Commercial-Residential, located on the east side of
Highway 260 in Heber-Overgaard and is surrounded by the same type of zoning, which is C-R
Commercial-Residential first 300 feet from Hwy 260 and R1-10 residential east of Buckskin
Road. 
Adjacent Land Uses and Zoning
North: C-R – Commercial and Residential Use
South: C-R - Vacant
East: Buckskin Road
West: Highway 260
The access to the site is off Highway 260 and will have egress on Buckskin Road. The facility
will have 25 RV sites for overnight camping with separate overnight parking. All will be open-air
storage with no cover at this time. There will be a bath house with two (2) restrooms and shower
accommodation, and a laundry room with washer and dryer. 
A ramada with picnic tables and BBQ grill will be placed for the visitors' use. An open space with
grass will be in the front, along HWY 260. LED Lighting will be installed for the safety of the
after-hours customers and will all be shielded downward, with reflections contained within the
property.
The size of spaces ranges from 30x59, 30x74, and 30x60 and each space will have water, sewer,
and power hook up.

<!-- page 204 -->
The business will be in operation 24/7, and the gate will remain open. There will be one (1)
employee during the day, but most of the business will be done online.
The site currently has trees, and they will be saved during the construction as much as possible for
landscaping purposes. The project will have a 6’ solid fence around the property. 
Signage, identifying the facility, will be placed in the front of the property.
The staff did not receive any comments from the public for or against to the project for P&Z
meeting but received one call and a letter before the BOS meeting. The neighbor's letter and the
applicant's response are provided under the attachments.
P&Z recommended approval of this item unanimously at their September 29, 2022, meeting with
no objections from the public.
Recommendation: 
The P&Z Commission and Staff recommend approval.

STAFF REPORT  
 
 
 
TO: Planning and Zoning Commission 
 
THROUGH:  Mischa Larisch, PW Deputy Director  
 
FROM: Berrin Nejad, Community Development Director 
  
HEARING DATE: September 29, 2022   
 
PROJECT NUMBER:   SUP 22-009 WOODY’S RV PARK AND STORAGE, DISTRICT 
IV: A request for a Special Use Permit to construct Overnight RV 
parking and RV storage with 25 overnight RV camping and RV storage 
area. The property is 4.30-acres in size, located east of State Route 260 
and west of Buckskin Road, in the Heber -Overgaard area. APN# 206-
02-012C, T12N, R17E, S30.  Owner: Larry Holland & Dianne Family 
Revocable Trust Applicant: Steve Wood. 
 
 
 
BACKGROUND and ANALYSIS: 
The applicant is requesting approval of a Special Use Permit for a n RV Park and storage facility.  
The property is zoned C -R Commercial -Residential and located east side of Highway 260 and 
bordered with Buckskin Road on the east side.  
  
LAND USE: 
Description and Character of Surrounding Area 
The site is on the east side of Highway 260 in Heber -Overgaard and the property is 
surrounded by the same type of zoning which is C-R Commercial-Residential first 300 feet 
from Hwy 260 and R1-10 residential east of Buckskin Road.

<!-- page 210 -->
Planning and Zoning Commission                                     September 29, 2022        2 | P a g e  
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 
Adjacent Land Uses and Zoning 
North:   C-R – Commercial and Residential Use 
South:   C-R - Vacant 
East:   Buckskin Road 
West:  Highway 260 
 
The access to the site is off Highway 260 and will have egress on Buckskin Road. The facility will 
have 25 RV sites for overnight camping with separate overnight parking , all will be an open -air 
storage with no cover at this time.  There will be a bath house with two (2) restrooms and shower 
accommodations, and a laundry room with washer and dryer.   
 
A ramada with picnic tables and BBQ grill  will be placed for the visitors use .  An open space with 
grass will be in the front, along the HWY 260.  LED Lighting will be installed for the safety of the 
afterhours customers and will all be shielded down ward, with reflections contained within the 
property.  
 
The size of spaces ranges from 30x59, 30x74, and 30x60 and each space will have water, sewer, and 
power hook up.  
 
The business will  be in operation 24/7, and the gate will remain open.  There will be one (1) 
employee during the day, but most of the business will be done online.  
 
The site currently has trees, and they will be saved during the construction as much as possible for 
the landscaping purposes.  The project will have 6’ solid fence around the property.    
 
A signage, identifying the facility, will be placed in the front of the property. 
 
The staff did not receive any comments from the public for or again st the proposal at the time staff 
report was prepared.
